## 2. Sub-Clause Evidence Analysis
(a) HOLDS, with a narrower 'iff' than stated. report() (:199-202) returns impl_->result only when completed; result with passed:true is constructed at the END of finish() (:173-181) and completed=true only at :182, so any earlier throw leaves no certificate. check_execution genuinely runs at construction (:96) and at finish() (:168). Evidence entries are compared by compare_tree with the exact tolerance max(1e-9, 1e-9*max(|a|,|e|)) at :44 (plus key-set equality :34, array length :38, Tagged/nonfinite rejection :46-47, exact equality for non-numerics :48): requested via full-object last_requested() wire :230 (wire shape {time_s,step,control,front_brake_demand,rear_brake_demand}, research.cpp:72-79, matching command_shapes input_detail.hpp:37) plus step :224 and time :226 pre-checks; applied via cursor-drained rows :113-121 with completeness :143; optional rider_commands (only when input.has_rider_commands, :126) via :125-138 comparing step and commands fields :134-135 (recorded folded rows are exactly {step,commands}, input.cpp:96) with completeness :144-145; transitions :234; observations at :101 (initial obs[0]) and :235 (obs[command+1] per command) covering all N+1 rows. mjSTATE_INTEGRATION uses the asymmetric numpy.allclose rule 1e-9 + 1e-9*|expected| at :63, applied to final state :167 and (unlisted) initial state :100. executable_sha256 is captured :97 and re-compared :169-170 -> IncompatibleExecution :170. Caveat: the stated condition list is a strict subset of the real gate - verify_configuration (:108 -> rebuild.cpp:180-214: configuration_sha256, terrain_sha256, per-vertex compiled terrain), outcome.reason/outcome :146-161, event metrics :162, research.{terminated,truncated,numerically_valid,max_energy_residual_ratio} :163-164, model_status :165, episode metrics :166, and the applied/rider_commands completeness counts also gate passed. The soundness direction (certificate => every listed check ran and passed) is fully supported; the 'iff' enumeration is non-exhaustive. Also note the recorded executable_sha256 is only emitted as recorded_executable_sha256 :179, never equated to the live image - cross-identity admission is the execution_identity axis instead. (b) PARTIALLY VIOLATED. Halt: the catch :241-252 sets p.mismatch for ANY std::exception inside the try (including IncompatibleExecution from the finish-time gate :168-170); every subsequent advance() throws 'replay stopped after mismatch' at :217 before any environment interaction, and no other member mutates env - pending()/snapshot()/render_frame()/diagnostics()/error()/timestep_s()/model_image() are read-only, close() only calls env->close(). restart() :254-261 is the sole escape, constructing a fresh Impl (new environment; old env closed :260); a failed restart leaves the old impl untouched (verified by test identity_recheck, tests/reference/test_replay_session.cpp:151-171). Non-halting throws: pre-try validation :215-216 (nonfinite/negative wall_budget, target behind committed step) and require_open :214 escape without setting mismatch - caller-contract violations, not replay failures; the session remains usable. Rollback: p.frame = p.verified :251 under a bumped presentation generation :250 (comment :247-249). VIOLATED SPECIFIC: on command==0 failure, frame() does NOT roll back to 'the valid initial t=0 environment->render_frame()' - it rolls back to a DEFAULT-CONSTRUCTED runtime::RenderFrame{}. The init list runs verified(frame) at :95 which copies frame BEFORE the ctor body assigns frame = environment->render_frame() at :102; verified receives a real frame only from capture_verified() (:139), first invoked at :239 after command 0 completes (or :182 inside finish). So for failures during command 0 - including the zero-command recording whose first advance() enters finish() at :220 - verified is {state.step=0, time_s=0, integration_state={}, model_sha256='', presentation=PresentationState{}, latest_sample=null}. This is a regression: pre-0c245f64 code ended the ctor with 'verified = frame' after the t=0 render; the start() extraction moved the init into the member list and silently changed the rollback target. Observable consequences: post-failure snapshot().integration_state is empty; frame.model_sha256 is ''; diagnostics().last_verified_step reports 0 (cosmetically correct, :209); and in --viewer mode the empty model digest trips viewer/ride.cpp:127-128 into 'viewer received a new model without an owned image supplier' (no hooks.model_image set, cli/replay.cpp:93-107), escaping to the outer catch with 'REPLAY ERROR: ...' on stderr :120 - masking the actual mismatch so session.diagnostics() is never emitted on that path. For command>=1 failures verified is a genuine post-command frame and the rollback is correct (verified by last_frame_unverified, test_replay_session.cpp:67-87). Note the environment itself is never rewound: failure_step takes environment->snapshot().step :245 (which can exceed the rolled-back frame step); rollback is presentation-only. (c) PARTIALLY VIOLATED. Halt: viewer hooks.advance catch-all stores failure (cli/replay.cpp:97-100); hooks.terminal (:95) returns done() || error().has_value(), and error() is set by the session's catch, so after the first throwing advance the viewer stops stepping; the checked_rollback exemption (viewer/ride.cpp:118) accepts the generation-bumped rollback frame without tripping the boundary invariant :119-120, and result.terminal latches at :123 (the window then sits in terminal hold until user close/interrupt, :159-160). Headless path exits its loop on the exception :109-110. Theoretical gap: an advance() throw that does not set mismatch (invalid args :215-216, closed :214) leaves error() empty -> terminal() false -> viewer would call advance again and re-throw indefinitely; unreachable from viewer::run's target computation (ride.cpp:106-115 always targets >= frame.step with a positive constant budget). Stream VIOLATION: 'prints non-empty failure diagnostics to stderr' is false for the advance-failure path - diagnostics are serialized to std::cout at :116 ('std::cout << io::recording_json(diagnostics)'), while stderr receives 'REPLAY ERROR: <what>' only from the outer catch :119-121, reachable for construction/setup/viewer-internal throws (e.g., missing recording -> exit 1 + REPLAY ERROR on stderr, asserted in tests/integration/test_replay_playback.cpp:125-127) but NOT for session.advance() failures, which are swallowed into 'failure' by the inner catch-alls :99/:110. Exception: the command-0 viewer defect from (b) makes viewer::run throw 'new model without owned image supplier', which does hit :120 with stderr + exit 1 - incidental and message-misleading, not failure diagnostics. Exit code: 'completed ? 0 : failed ? 1 : 2' :118 - any advance failure yields 1 (failed = error() || static_cast<bool>(failure), :114); interrupt overrides to 130 :117; user-close without failure returns 2; ArgumentError maps to exit 2 at dispatch (application.cpp:23-24).

## 3. Exceptions, Dependencies & Consequences
Exception taxonomy: (1) MismatchFailure (session.cpp:16-26, recording::RecordingError subclass, carries first_path/max_absolute_error) - the only throw that populates first_mismatch :246 and diagnostics' first_mismatch_field :210. (2) recording::RecordingError for cursor/ordering/completeness violations and 'command N: episode ended early or step does not align' :225 - treated identically to mismatch (sets p.mismatch, halts) but leaves first_mismatch null. (3) IncompatibleExecution (session.hpp:14-22) - at construction propagates out of the ctor so no session exists (check() maps to status 'incompatible-execution' :279-281); thrown inside finish() :168-170 it is caught by :241 and recorded as a plain 'mismatch' halt with status 'mismatch' in diagnostics :206. (4) std::invalid_argument/std::runtime_error from pre-try validation :214-216 - do NOT set mismatch or halt; the attempt remains live. (5) std::overflow_error for generation exhaustion in restart :257 and the catch's generation guard :250. (6) std::runtime_error 'replay session is closed' via require_open :112 (checked at :214, :194-198, :255). (7) environment-internal exceptions (research.cpp:176-177 'window already active', :351 'begin_control must precede', invalid brake :178-179 etc.) - caught :241 -> halt. Dependencies: halt depends only on impl_->mismatch, never on the environment's own state; the env is NOT rolled back after failure - it keeps whatever prefix advance_control committed, and p.failure_step records environment->snapshot().step best-effort :245 (may exceed the presentation frame's step). verified rollback is presentation-only: frame/verified are owned copies; environment->render_frame() is called for fresh frames at :102/:139/:233. The yield path :233 lets frame run ahead of verified mid-window, which is exactly what the generation-bumped rollback rewinds; begin_control'd-but-unverified commands are never ack'd post-failure (delivery_pending can remain latched in the env until close/restart). restart() is a wholesale reset (new Impl, new env, fresh input re-load re-running check_execution - drift in the recording fails at :258-259 or inside the new ctor, leaving the old impl intact); the generation is reserved so one rollback bump can't collide :256-257. Consequences: (i) the command-0 default-verified defect yields an empty integration_state/model_sha256 frame and, under --viewer, a spurious 'new model without owned image supplier' REPLAY ERROR on stderr that hides the real mismatch diagnostics; (ii) certificate requires the execution image on disk unchanged for the whole playback :97/:169-170 (rebuild mid-replay aborts); (iii) legacy schema-1 inputs and any identity-axis drift are refused before plant construction (rebuild.cpp:216-218); (iv) close() is idempotent and leaves diagnostics available but report()/snapshot()/render_frame()/model_image()/timestep_s() throwing on closed.

## 4. Test Coverage & Gaps
COVERED. native/tests/replay/test_session.cpp: comparator unit checks (tolerance :20-21, first_path/max_absolute_error :23, bool-vs-int :24, NaN-never-certifies :26), contract-v1 rejection :38-40, real round-trip: zero-budget yield keeps pending control :140, early close -> report() refuses + diagnostics passed=false :141-143, completed replay passed :144-146, restart step=0 + generation bump :147-148, command-0 tampered transition -> rejects + error() + first_mismatch_field non-null + snapshot().step==0 && generation>0 :155-166. tests/reference/test_replay_session.cpp: headless/incremental equivalence with check() + zero/target-step yields + restart :15-45; early_close :48-62; last_frame_unverified (mid-run rollback to post-command verified frame under new generation after finish's final-state mismatch) :67-87; semantic_tampers x3 (applied step, transitions physics_steps, observations time_s rejected with named labels) :100-123; identity gates before plant :130-142 and at restart boundary keeping the old verified frame un-advanced :151-171; mismatch_refusal - post-mismatch advance throws 'replay stopped after mismatch' and commits no further physics :172-192 (halt permanence). tests/reference/test_native_replay_session.cpp: same-backend restart reaches identical certificate :22-48; gate invoked with recorded identity :54-65; foreign identity rejected pre-plant :70-85. tests/reference/test_native_platform_replay.cpp: same-host pass :49-56; foreign recording/runtime refused by named field :62-98; drift recheck at restart :106-118; foreign recording still plays via --view and inspection paths never invoke the gate :123-194. tests/integration/test_replay_playback.cpp: --view --dry-run exit 0 :115-119; missing recording -> exit 1 + 'REPLAY ERROR' on stderr :125-127; default checked path verifies sealed container to exit 0 :133-153. tests/reference/test_ride_backend_cli.cpp:247-267: --time-scale without --viewer -> ArgumentError -> exit 2 through the produced binary. GAPS. (1) No test distinguishes the command-0 rollback frame's CONTENT: tamper_roundtrip (test_session.cpp:165) and mismatch_refusal (test_replay_session.cpp:186-189) assert only snapshot().step==0/unchanged - satisfied by the default-constructed RenderFrame{}; nothing asserts post-failure snapshot().integration_state non-empty or frame.model_sha256 equality, so the verified=default regression (current session.cpp:95 vs pre-0c245f64 'verified = frame') is invisible. (2) No test drives the bike-replay binary through a mid-run mismatch to pin stdout-vs-stderr routing or exit code 1 for that path; mismatch coverage is at the replay::check()/session API level, while binary coverage is only missing-recording (stderr) and success (exit 0). (3) No headless test of the checked-replay viewer loop: hooks.advance catch->failure->terminal halt and the checked_rollback boundary exemption (viewer/ride.cpp:118) are exercised only interactively; the command-0 'model supplier' crash path is unobserved. (4) The mid-playback executable_sha256 change (session.cpp:169-170) is structurally untestable in-process (static image identity is immutable per test comments). (5) diagnostics() fields are only partially asserted (first_mismatch_field non-null; last_verified_step/first_mismatch_step values unpinned). (6) rider_commands channel compare (:125-138) is only reachable for contract-3 containers that own channel_rider_commands (input.cpp:164-166); the skip branch is implicitly covered by legacy tests but the row-compare semantics (step+commands only) lack a tamper test.

## 5. Recommended Refinement
Fix: restore the pre-0c245f64 semantics by making verified hold the validated t=0 frame - set 'verified = frame' (or invoke capture_verified()) after 'frame = environment->render_frame(); frame.state.generation = generation;' at native/src/replay/session.cpp:102; the ctor already verifies initial integration state (:100) and observations[0] (:101), so the t=0 boundary is legitimately 'verified'. Optionally set hooks.model_image in cli/replay.cpp so a rollback never strands the viewer, and/or guard the empty-frame case. Property wording: (a) either enumerate the full gate (verify_configuration, initial state+observation, outcome/event-metrics/research fields/model_status/episode metrics, applied and rider_commands completeness, step/time alignment) or restate as 'passed attests that the complete recorded-evidence battery ran and matched, including ...'. (b) keep the FailedHalted + verified-frame clauses but change the parenthetical to 'the default-constructed RenderFrame{} (step 0, empty integration state/model digest) when failure occurs on command == 0' to match implementation, or mark it as the defect it is relative to intent. (c) replace 'prints non-empty failure diagnostics to stderr' with the actual contract: on advance() failure the session diagnostics JSON is printed to stdout and the command exits 1 (130 on interrupt, 2 on user-close without failure); 'REPLAY ERROR:' on stderr is reserved for exceptions escaping the inner handling (construction/setup/viewer-internal throws). Tests to add: (1) command-0 mismatch asserting snapshot().integration_state non-empty and render_frame().model_sha256 equals the session model digest - would catch the verified-init regression; (2) binary-level mid-run mismatch run pinning stdout diagnostics + empty stderr + exit 1 for the headless path; (3) a synthetic/headless viewer-loop test for the catch->terminal halt and the checked_rollback exemption; (4) diagnostics() field assertions for last_verified_step/first_mismatch_step; (5) a rider_commands-channel tamper case for contract-3 containers.

# Stage 3 (`R1`) Independent Research Report — `P19`: Viewer Replica Isolation, Monotone `(generation, step)` Presentation, Pacing & GPU Destruction Order

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Compliance Verdict (`R1`):** `holds`
- **Product Justification Verdict (`R1`):** `relevant_confirmed_requirement`

## 1. Producers, Mutators, Consumers & Potential Breakers
PRODUCERS of runtime::RenderFrame (owned value, runtime.hpp:94-100): (1) NativeRideRuntime::render_frame() — runtime/runtime.cpp:872-884, copies the committed cache under one AdvancementGuard (integration_state, presentation, generation=physical_->generation() :877, outcome, latest_sample, first_failure) and stamps model_image_.sha256; (2) NativeResearchRuntime::render_frame() — runtime/research.cpp:412-418, delegates and overrides state.outcome with episode reason; (3) replay::ReplaySession — replay/session.cpp:195 returns owned impl_->frame, captured at :102 (ctor), :139 capture_verified(), :233 pending-yield refresh, :251 verified-rollback restore, with generation restamped at :102/:139/:233/:251; (4) replay::PlaybackSession::frame() — replay/playback.cpp:207-243, stored-state presentation via engine::reset_data+qpos/qvel writes+engine::get_state only (:216-225), step=keyframe index (:223), no controller/policy/integration (:220-221 comment); cli/replay.cpp:44-50 restamps step=tick/time_s for the inspection cursor; (5) viewer::capture_frame() — viewer/snapshot.cpp:9-26, owned helper (get_state INTEGRATION + six model fields). GENERATION MUTATORS: NativeRideRuntime::reset() bumps physical_->set_generation(next) with INT_MAX guard (runtime.cpp:959-979; generations start at 1 — bootstrap.cpp:405-408, accounting.cpp:440); ReplaySession mismatch rollback ++generation (session.cpp:247-251) and restart() uses generation+1 reserving a rollback slot (:254-261); cli/replay.cpp rebase() ++generation on seek/loop/reset (:39-42, :57, :63, :67, :71). CONSUMERS: RenderOwner::apply/draw (render_owner.cpp:68-144); viewer HUD/overlay (viewer/hud.cpp:55-156); hooks.presented → PhysicalLiveCsv preview rows (ride.cpp:147 → cli/ride.cpp:132-135 → research/preview_csv.cpp:67-118, generation change emits 'reset' event row :100-101); preview_row (cli/ride.cpp:32-51); viz/* consume only the downstream recorded columns (viz/ride.cpp:16-39 evidence_markers renders RESET/outcome markers). BOUNDARY GUARDS / BREAKERS: RenderOwner::apply validation wall (render_owner.cpp:70-88) throws before the staged copy (:89-90) so rejections leave the last accepted replica intact; run-loop advance-boundary check throws on generation change / step regression / step>target except inspection wrap and checked_replay rollback (ride.cpp:117-120); clock_generation monotone guard throws 'viewer generation moved backwards' (ride.cpp:132-134); digest change requires hooks.model_image supplier else throw (ride.cpp:127-128); pacer.completed(actual>offered) throws (pacer.cpp:56); GlfwContext callback failures are captured and rethrown on poll() (glfw_context.cpp:48-51,:120).

## 2. Sub-Clause Evidence Analysis
(a) HOLDS. RenderOwner::apply (render_owner.cpp:68-110) validates model_sha256==impl_->digest (:72) and enforces lexicographic monotone non-decreasing (generation, step): rejects generation<0, step<0, and any regression vs the last accepted frame (:73-76) — same-generation step decrease or generation decrease throw 'render frame moved backwards without a generation change'; equal (gen,step) re-applies are admitted (needed for repeated draws/overlay refresh), and gen increases may land on any step ≥0 (reset → step 0, test_render.cpp:90-92). Kinematic-only is structural: all validation precedes 'auto staged = frame' (:90), then engine::invoke runs exactly mj_setState+mj_kinematics+mj_comPos+mj_camlight+mj_tendon (:98-105) — no mj_step/mj_forward/sensors/qfrc force writers/policy; comment :96-97 states the contract. The six replica model arrays (dof_frictionloss, site_pos, tendon_{stiffness,damping,lengthspring,range}; render_fields.hpp:14-21) are written per frame into the OWNED replica only (:92-93) — these are the declared mutable presentation fields per RIDE.md:216-219, not live force accounting. Replica owns its model+data loaded from the verified ModelImage (ctor :44-66, digest check :45-46); engine failure poisons further use (:38,:106). Isolation proven by test_physical_view.cpp:84-115 (source snapshot byte-identical across applies) and test_render.cpp:53-61 (mutating source model/data post-capture leaves replica intact). (b) HOLDS. In viewer::run, GlfwContext window is declared first (ride.cpp:31) and unique_ptr<RenderOwner> second (:32): exception unwind destroys renderer before window, and ~RenderOwner→close() (render_owner.cpp:67,165-171) runs mjr_freeContext/mjv_freeScene (:169) while the GLFW window and its current GL context (made current at glfw_context.cpp:78, re-currented by framebuffer_size() :138) are still alive; the normal path additionally calls renderer->close() explicitly at ride.cpp:166 before 'return result' → ~GlfwContext→~Impl destroys GLFWwindow and calls glfwTerminate only when the last registered context goes away (glfw_context.cpp:57-60). Vacuous cases also safe: GlfwContext ctor throw precedes any RenderOwner; RenderOwner ctor/initial apply throw destroys the null/partial unique_ptr before window teardown. close() is noexcept, idempotent (:167) and hard-stops off-thread via std::terminate (:168); GlfwContext dtor likewise terminates off the application main thread (:113-115, main-thread pin :18-26 incl. macOS pthread_main_np). Caveat: correct ordering is structurally guaranteed but only executable under BIKE_BUILD_GUI=ON; qualified test lanes compile OFF (test_frontend_windows.cpp:5-6 asserts this). (c) HOLDS. Pacer fixes dt at construction (pacer.cpp:11-12, validated finite/positive :18-21) and never scales it: scale only multiplies accrued wall debt (debt += elapsed*scale :41) while raw_steps = floor(debt/dt) (:45); offered = paused?0:min(raw_steps, max_offer=1_000_000) (:49-51) so offered<=max_offer always and completed(actual>offered) throws (:56) while debiting only actual steps×dt (:57-58), preserving the uncompleted remainder for cooperative budget yields (comment :46-48). Stale debt is discarded on every discontinuity: set_scale/set_paused zero debt+offered and arm rebase_pending consumed at next poll (:23-29,:40), reset() clears everything (:30-33), and the run loop calls pacer.reset on reset (:69), observed generation/step changes (:94-98), and generation bumps (:136). Scale domain {1,2,4,8} enforced by speed_for_action validation inside set_scale (:24 → input.cpp:33-42; scale 3 rejected, tested test_hud.cpp:40). terminal_drawn (ride.cpp:45) arms request_frame once when result.terminal first holds (:138) so the full draw block — apply+overlay+draw+swap+presented (:142-149) — executes before the close_on_terminal break (:159); re-armed on reset (:64). First poll also always requests the initial frame (pacer.cpp:36). AchievedRate enforces monotone finite wall+sim clocks (:63-72, 0.5 s window) and is bypassed entirely in inspection mode whose seek cursor is not an integrated clock (ride.cpp:139-141). (d) HOLDS. Legend latching (ride.cpp:150-157): legend_generation (init -1, :41) makes the first telemetry tick print a legend; reprints happen only on generation change and only for modes physical/research/checked_replay (:151-152) — inspection's presentation clock never emits a physical legend; the HUD line itself prints on generation change OR ≥0.5 s cadence (:150,:157) via hooks.hud override or hud_line; [FIRST FAILURE] is latched per distinct message in reported_failure (:153-155) and cleared on generation change (:135) so it re-announces per episode — matching RIDE.md:124-126. Reset hooks (ride.cpp:60-71): hooks.reset() seals/drains the old episode per session.hpp:21-24 contract, then input.apply(reset) clears paused/braking (input.cpp:53), hooks.brakes(0.,0.) (:66) and hooks.pause(false) (:67) re-sync caller-side demand/queue state — without them the captured front/rear demands (cli/ride.cpp:128,:136-137) and a research pause would leak into the new episode. Journal semantics preserved: cli/ride.cpp:137-138 emits 'brakes'/{0,0} and 'resume' rows at post-reset step 0 in the new episode journal; research.cpp:18-20 → PolicySession records operator_requested/applied events (policy_session.cpp:86-108,:264).

## 3. Exceptions, Dependencies & Consequences
DEPENDENCIES: main-thread confinement everywhere — RenderOwner::Impl::thread captured at construction (render_owner.cpp:25, checked :36, terminate in close() :168); GlfwContext pins the loading-TU main thread (glfw_context.cpp:18-26) with macOS pthread_main_np verification; BIKE_BUILD_GUI compile gate bifurcates every GL path (glfw_context.cpp:8-14, render_owner.cpp:115-143, ride.cpp:16-21). EXCEPTION SEMANTICS: RenderOwner poisons permanently on engine failure (render_owner.cpp:38,:106,:140); all apply validation is pre-mutation so rejected frames keep the last accepted replica (:89-90, proven test_render.cpp:86-88, test_physical_view.cpp:145-163); close() noexcept+idempotent (:167-171); GlfwContext callback exceptions defer to poll() rethrow (glfw_context.cpp:48-51,:120). CONSEQUENCES/EDGE CASES: (1) monotone check admits generation SKIPS (gen 0→5) — only regression is forbidden, consistent with replay reserving a rollback generation (session.cpp:256-258); (2) debt accrual is honest wall debt, not capped — a stall leaves offered ≤1e6 steps per poll but the viewer keeps catching up until debt drains (deliberate per pacer.cpp:46-48); (3) on re-observed external step change the loop rebases BEFORE computing a new target (ride.cpp:91-98) — conservative choice discards drain-completed work rather than risking double-counted debt; (4) inspection_wrap debits the full requested count (cursor owner consumed all ticks, cli/replay.cpp:51-59) while checked_rollback debits 0 (rollback discarded the work, debt preserved for the restored verified frame) — ride.cpp:117-121; (5) model-digest change rebuilds RenderOwner via hooks.model_image with apply BEFORE swap so a bad replacement cannot displace the live replica (ride.cpp:127-131); (6) PlaybackClock (policy_session.hpp:58-76, lifecycle.cpp:263-292) is a SECOND pacing implementation with different semantics (debt capped at max(.05*scale,dt) s, sim-second clock inputs, int64 target returns) — documented in NATIVE_POLICIES.md:94 but has NO production caller; only tests exercise it (test_lifecycle.cpp:91-105, test_playback_clock.cpp); divergence is contractual, not a bug, but the two coexisting pacing models are a maintenance hazard; (7) viewer::run_ride (ride.cpp:170-189) is dead code — zero call sites in native/src or tests (acknowledged pending removal in docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-16-audit-fixes.md task 7.10); (8) hooks.pause(false)/hooks.brakes(0,0) emit journal rows unconditionally on reset — deterministic normalization of post-reset operator state (cli/ride.cpp:137-138,:147); (9) 'force writers' literal reading: apply writes dof_frictionloss/tendon params into the OWNED replica model — permitted under the documented six-array presentation contract (RIDE.md:216-219) but worth pinning in the property wording.

## 4. Test Coverage & Gaps
COVERED: viewer_render (native/tests/viewer/test_render.cpp) — owned capture extents (:44-46), apply+isolation (:48-61), all six field updates (:63-74), same-generation step regression rejected (:75), digest mismatch (:76-77), field/state width mismatch (:78-81), time-consistency (:82-83), nonfinite endpoint (:84-85), rejection preserves replica (:86-88), new generation permits step 0 (:90-92), markers/camera/draw(0,0) (:93-95), thread confinement (:96-102), idempotent close (:103-104), image digest ctor check (:105-106). viewer_hud (native/tests/viewer/test_hud.cpp) — Pacer init poll, debt accrual, completed() debit+yield preservation, over-completion throw, scale rebase discarding debt, pause accrual freeze + no resume burst, backward-clock throw, set_scale(3) throw, ctor validation, reset clears debt (:17-44); AchievedRate window/regression (:46-51); action mapping incl. F6/F7/F8 and mode-specific Space/B/,/V, InputState reset clearing pause/braking while preserving scale, camera presets, hud_legend Unicode width, replay_exit_code (:53-118). test_physical_view.cpp — kinematic-only isolation vs live runtime (:84-115), frame ownership across reset+close (:122-133), reject-before-mutation (:138-166). test_native_viewer_snapshot.cpp — owned frame/replica scribble (:152-178), target-slice yield semantics (:183-219). test_frontend_windows.cpp — GUI-off gate, pacer render-interval gate >=1/60 (:80-90), viewer-construction-failure accounting (:122-203). PlaybackClock ports (test_playback_clock.cpp, test_lifecycle.cpp:91-105). close_on_finish→close_on_terminal wiring (test_capture.cpp:41-66). replay session lifecycle incl. close/restart (test_session.cpp, test_replay_session.cpp). GAPS: (1) no automated run of viewer::run's loop against a live GlfwContext — qualified lanes compile BIKE_BUILD_GUI=OFF (gui preset exists, CMakePresets.json:53-59, but test_frontend_windows.cpp:130 asserts !gui_available(), i.e. the interactive loop itself is never executed in tests); consequently (b) real destruction order, terminal_drawn's post-terminal render pass, clock_generation regression throw (ride.cpp:133), advance-boundary throw (:119-120), model replacement (:127-131), in-loop legend latch (:150-157), and reset-hook calls (:66-67) are source-verified only; (2) max_offer=1e6 cap never exercised (needs debt >1e6 steps, ~156 s wall at scale 8 — trivially testable via a large fake clock jump); (3) RenderOwner::draw GL path untested (only the 0x0 early-return); (4) ReplaySession mismatch-rollback generation bump → viewer acceptance path untested end-to-end; (5) render-owner replacement while graphics initialized untested.

## 5. Recommended Refinement
Refine the statement to: '(a) RenderOwner::apply is evaluation-kinematic-only — mj_setState+mj_kinematics+mj_comPos+mj_camlight+mj_tendon with no forward/step/sensor/qfrc evaluation — while writing only the six declared mutable presentation arrays of its own replica model, and enforces monotone non-decreasing lexicographic (generation, step) admission plus model_sha256 identity, with all rejection staged before replica mutation' — matches code exactly and resolves the 'force writers' ambiguity (dof_frictionloss IS a presentation-mutable array). For (b), cite the dual mechanism: reverse declaration order at ride.cpp:31-32 for unwind plus explicit renderer->close() at :166 for the normal path. For (c), clarify 'caps offered ≤ max_offer=1e6 physics steps per poll' is an offer bound, not a debt bound (debt is honest and uncapped by design), and note Pacer is the interactive loop's clock while research::PlaybackClock is a separate, test-only pacing implementation with a 50 ms×scale debt cap — the property should name viewer::Pacer explicitly to avoid conflating them. Verification additions: a hidden-window GUI smoke test (GlfwContext hidden=true) exercising run() with a scripted terminal+close_on_terminal to prove destruction order and terminal_drawn at runtime; a Pacer unit asserting poll(t+200 s, scale=8).physics_steps==1e6; and removal or wiring of dead viewer::run_ride (pending plan task 7.10).

# Stage 3 (`R1`) Independent Research Report — `P20`: Batch Worker-Pool Safety, Subprocess Group Lifecycle, CLI Cross-Mode Validation & Validation-Suite Lattice

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Compliance Verdict (`R1`):** `partially_violated`
- **Product Justification Verdict (`R1`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Producers, Mutators, Consumers & Potential Breakers
PRODUCERS: batch::execute jobs come from batch::expand_spec(parse_spec_toml) (native/src/cli/batch.cpp:25-28) and from research_sweep's per-seed Job construction (native/src/batch/sweep.cpp:38-48, argv built at :42, binary inserted at :46); test-side Job producers at native/tests/batch/test_plan.cpp:146-161. Results produced inside workers at process_pool.cpp:52-66 (result.exit_code/out/err/summary written outside any lock; tail_json allocates at :61). publication index produced under mutex at process_pool.cpp:68. platform::run produces ProcessResult via posix_spawnp spawn (native/src/platform/process.cpp:240-245) and the drain loop (process.cpp:295-384); the ChildOwner RAII guard is constructed immediately after spawn at process.cpp:248-262. MUTATORS: worker lambda mutates results[index] (process_pool.cpp:52-66), atomic next cursor (:36,:51), stop_source (:37,:73), completed[]/published under mutex (:68), fatal exception_ptr under mutex (:72); main thread mutates received at :86. platform::run mutates poll/reap state: open_fds/reaped/killed/overflowed/cancellation_sent (process.cpp:288-292), killpg escalation state (:297-307,:364-367), and child.released at :386. CLI mutators: Arguments::set in sweep option parsing (sweep.cpp:17,31), validate_research_arguments throws (configuration.cpp:172-191), resolve_ride cross-mode gates (configuration.cpp:136-161), batch job argv mutation (cli/batch.cpp:50-54). CONSUMERS: options.completed callback consumes (index,results[index]) after lock release at process_pool.cpp:84-87; cli::batch_command's callback appends batch_results.jsonl + per-job logs (cli/batch.cpp:60-68); research_sweep's callback writes per-seed logs + rc lines (sweep.cpp:60-63); batch::summarize consumes the results vector (cli/batch.cpp:76). platform::run consumed by batch::execute (process_pool.cpp:59), by test harnesses (tests/integration/test_batch.cpp:220,255,280,334; tests/platform/test_primitives.cpp:107-213), and by replay/research test drivers. RESTORE/WIRE BOUNDARIES: InterruptShield deferred-SIGINT wiring in batch_command (cli/batch.cpp:56,59,78) and research_sweep (sweep.cpp:57,59,71); stop_token relay across pool boundary (process_pool.cpp:38,48,58); spawn_mutex serialization boundary for non-atomic pipe(O_CLOEXEC) platforms (process.cpp:191-195, unlocked at :269); killpg boundary uses child pid as pgid via POSIX_SPAWN_SETPGROUP+setpgroup(0) (process.cpp:209-212); report write boundary is write_report/write_report_text with staged exclusive file + rename + sync_directory (evidence.cpp:67-90); validation case dispatch boundary run_case -> case_* handlers (registry.cpp:112-114,145,199,203-204,222,225-246). BREAKERS (observed defects): (1) child.released set at process.cpp:386, not at reap :310-311 — post-reap exceptions still enter ~ChildOwner killpg+waitpid (:252-257); (2) sweep.cpp calls cli::parse_arguments at :45 but never cli::validate_research_arguments; (3) sweep --jobs clamped via std::max(1,...) at :35 while sweep.json records the raw value at :69; (4) source_fingerprint extension allowlist (evidence.cpp:96) excludes .inc/.mm/.cu/.in — argument_contracts.inc and catalog_data.inc edits are invisible; (5) drivetrain.cpp:121, scenarios.cpp:133-139, experiments.cpp:262-268 (reference_matrix) and :274-281 (contact_resolution) capture source_sha256 without a post-run unchanged gate.

## 2. Sub-Clause Evidence Analysis
(a) batch::execute — HOLDS. Worker join discipline: normal path joins every joinable jthread at process_pool.cpp:95 before returning at :96; the catch at :90-94 requests stop, joins all, and rethrows; the launch loop :77 sits inside the try :76 so a throwing emplace_back still joins already-launched workers (jthread dtors are a second belt). Exact job-index order: results is preallocated at :33 and ids pre-stamped at :35; each worker writes only results[index] fetched from the atomic cursor :51-52; completed[] records completion order for callbacks only (:43,:68,:86-87). No heap allocation under mutex: the entire critical section at :68 is `completed[published++]=index` on a vector pre-sized to jobs.size() at :43 (comment :41-42 documents the contract); callbacks run after lock.unlock() at :84-87; the fatal path captures std::current_exception() under the same mutex at :72 which does not allocate on the Itanium ABI. Memory ordering is sound: result fields are written before the mutex release (:67 comment) and readers observe the index only through the locked publication. Cancellation: external options.cancellation relays into stop (:38,:48); queued jobs get exit_code=130 'cancelled before worker launch' (:56); running children receive stop.get_token() (:58) -> platform::run SIGINT. (b) platform::run — MOSTLY HOLDS, one literal violation. Portability: add_chdir dispatches on __APPLE__ (SDK>=26 -> POSIX.1-2024 name, else _np, process.cpp:114-121) and glibc __GLIBC_PREREQ(2,40) (:122-128); both stated platforms compile their branch. Caveats: __MAC_OS_X_VERSION_MAX_ALLOWED keys on the SDK ceiling, not __MAC_OS_X_VERSION_MIN_REQUIRED, so an SDK>=26 build targeting an older macOS selects a symbol missing on the target (weak-import crash or -Wunguarded-availability) — mitigated only because the project sets no CMAKE_OSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET; and the #else fallback (:129-131) unconditionally uses the POSIX name, so non-glibc Linux (musl) would not compile — outside the stated assumption but unguarded. Pipe drain: single poll loop drains both read ends (:320-349), closes on got==0 (EOF) or POLLNVAL (:375-382), correctly reads pending data ahead of a bare POLLHUP (:372-374), and exits only when reaped && open_fds==0 (:318) — EOF semantics hold. Escalation: cancellation -> killpg(pid,SIGINT) once (:297) with deadline=min(deadline,now+grace) (:299-300, grace validated nonnegative at :171-172); deadline or output overflow -> killpg(pid,SIGKILL) (:304-307,:364-367); child leads its own process group (SETPGROUP+setpgroup(0), :209-212). Input validation is invalid_argument before any spawn (:144-173); pre-spawn cancellation returns exit_code 130 (:189). VIOLATION of the released-timing letter: waitpid WNOHANG sets the local `reaped` flag at :310-311 but child.released is only assigned at :386 after the drain loop; an exception in the post-reap window (poll :347, read :358, sink allocation :369, waitpid :315) unwinds into ~ChildOwner (:252-257) which calls killpg(id,SIGKILL)+blocking waitpid on a reaped pid. The re-waitpid cannot hang (ECHILD returns immediately), but killpg can hit a recycled pgid — bounded by the drain window, real hazard, and exactly what the property wording prohibits. (c) CLI/batch cross-mode — PARTIALLY VIOLATED. bike-batch is fully fail-closed: --jobs validated `count<1||>UINT_MAX` at cli/batch.cpp:24 before the spec is read (:25) and before output creation (:42-43); every expanded job runs parse_arguments("bike-research") + validate_research_arguments at :31-36 before dry-run/output/dispatch; output and per-job collisions rejected at :42,:52. bike-research direct: research.cpp:207 -> resolve_research -> validate_research_arguments (configuration.cpp:196) enumerates cross-mode conflicts (:173-190) before any environment work. bike-ride: resolve_ride gates experiment-only options, time-scale/close-on-finish/preview/record/check-policy/duration/speed/rider combinations (configuration.cpp:136-161). bike-replay: --time-scale requires --viewer and --viewer XOR --view before session construction (replay.cpp:84-85). dispatch maps ArgumentError/invalid_argument to exit 2 (application.cpp:23-28). bike-research-sweep, however: (i) calls only cli::parse_arguments at sweep.cpp:45 and never validate_research_arguments — semantic conflicts (e.g. --demand+--demand-file, --rider-program conflicts, diagnostic_model_limits+strict from configuration.cpp:173-176) pass the parent screen and are caught only inside each spawned child via research_command -> resolve_research (configuration.cpp:196) exiting 2; the net system still fails closed end-to-end (rc -> sweep.json -> exit 1 at :69-71) but the output root was already created at :56, so 'before dispatch or output creation' fails at the letter; (ii) --jobs is clamped, not rejected: std::max<int64_t>(1,args.integer("jobs")) at :35 turns --jobs 0/-N into 1 worker, and sweep.json even records the raw value (:69), versus bike-batch's hard rejection — a fail-open normalization, though execute() would still refuse workers==0 (process_pool.cpp:32). Unknown sweep options do throw (:29) and --out/--seeds are required (:33). (d) validation lattice — PARTIALLY VIOLATED. Checkpoints/partial runs: write_report stages+renames atomically (evidence.cpp:67-90); reports initialize passed:false and rewrite after each unit — benchmark_suite per case (benchmark.cpp:240,244,251), climbing_benchmark per repetition (:285,288,290), mechanics_report per check (:267,269,271), fidelity_sweep per variant (experiments.cpp:110,149), drive_suspension_comparison checkpoint() (drivetrain.cpp:111,115,121), reference_matrix per scenario (experiments.cpp:262-268); interrupted runs therefore persist a last checkpoint with passed:false — fail-closed. benchmark_suite also rejects jobs!=1 rather than silently serializing (:223-227) and records complete_suite/required_case_count (:238,242). Synthetic data: validate_dataset throws unless allow_synthetic (tire.cpp:223-224; opt-in via registry.cpp:112 boolean flag, default false); is_synthetic covers the field and synthetic/simulation:/fixture: prefixes (:176-181); accepted_calibration_status demotes to parameterized_unvalidated (:183-185); calibration_report emits measured_holdout_passed only for non-synthetic passing holdout (:291); write_release_manifest requires explicit synthetic:false, rejects synthetic/failed-holdout payloads and 'validated' filenames for unvalidated evidence (acceptance.cpp:164-176); validate_split rejects fit/holdout overlap (tire.cpp:152-156, registry.cpp:145). Source-edit detection: unchanged gating exists in climbing_repetition (benchmark.cpp:184-187 feeding suite passed :290), benchmark_suite mechanics/release gates (:247-250 — though `passed` itself at :246-249 does NOT include unchanged), fidelity_sweep (experiments.cpp:146-147), mechanics_report (:270-271). BUT the fingerprint allowlist (evidence.cpp:96: .cpp/.hpp/.h/.cmake/CMakeLists.txt) excludes native/src/cli/argument_contracts.inc and native/src/recording/catalog_data.inc (plus gpu/metal_batch_runner.mm, gpu/cuda_kernels.cu, platform/numerics_symbols.hpp.in) — mid-run edits to compiled-in include files are invisible to every gate, directly contradicting 'including .inc headers'. Additionally drive_suspension_comparison, scenario runs, reference_matrix and contact_resolution capture source_sha256 for provenance but never re-compare it (drivetrain.cpp:107 vs :121; scenarios.cpp:133 vs :136-139; experiments.cpp:262 vs :267; :274 vs :279-281), so the lattice's edit-detection coverage is partial by design, not universal.

## 3. Exceptions, Dependencies & Consequences
EXCEPTIONS: batch::execute — worker per-job throws are converted to exit_code=-1 results (process_pool.cpp:63-65); anything escaping the job loop (e.g. stop_callback registration or allocation inside the inner catch) is captured once as `fatal` under mutex (:71-74), wakes the consumer (:82), is rethrown at :83, and the outer catch (:90-94) requests stop and joins all workers before propagating — join-before-destruction preserved on every path; options.completed throwing at :87 takes the same join path. platform::run — input-domain violations are std::invalid_argument pre-spawn (:144-173); OS failures std::system_error via fail_errno (:38-41); output overflow kills the group, finishes draining to EOF, then throws std::runtime_error naming the bound (:362-370,:386-390); destructor ~ChildOwner (:252-257) is noexcept-safe (killpg SIGKILL + waitpid with EINTR retry) — but see the post-reap-window caveat: released at :386 vs reap at :310 leaves a window where the destructor acts on a possibly-recycled pgid. DEPENDENCIES: POSIX-only surface (process.hpp:8) — posix_spawnp, POSIX_SPAWN_SETPGROUP, killpg, poll, waitpid, _NSGetEnviron on Apple (process.cpp:134-142); chdir portability depends on Apple SDK>=26 vs _np and glibc>=2.40 vs _np (:114-132) with the musl #else edge; spawn_mutex exists only because pipe()+fcntl(FD_CLOEXEC) is non-atomic on some targets (:191-195); cancellation grace uses steady_clock deadline arithmetic (:271-275,:299-300). CLI chain depends on cli::parse_arguments grammar + validate_research_arguments semantics being invoked as a pair — batch.cpp does both (:34), sweep.cpp does only the first (:45). source_fingerprint depends on filesystem enumeration of native/src plus the extension allowlist (evidence.cpp:94-96) and on the caller choosing to re-compare — half the lattice only captures it. CONSEQUENCES: (1) sweep --jobs 0 silently runs 1 worker and records jobs:0 in sweep.json — an inaccurate provenance record, though never a 0-worker dispatch; (2) sweep passthrough semantic conflicts create the output root and per-seed logs before failing inside children — exit code still fails closed; (3) .inc/.mm/.cu/.in edits mid-run are undetectable — a report can carry source_unchanged_during_run:true and even passed:true over edited compiled inputs; (4) benchmark_suite `passed` does not include unchanged — only mechanics_gate_passed/release_gate_passed do (:246-250); (5) the post-reap ~ChildOwner killpg is a small-window stray-signal hazard, not a hang; (6) macOS SDK-vs-deployment-target mismatch is latent only — correct on qualified builds since no deployment target is configured.

## 4. Test Coverage & Gaps
COVERED. (a) native/tests/batch/test_plan.cpp:148-164: serial vs 2-worker execute produce identical ordered results and preserved exit codes (:149-150), literal argv incl. unicode/metacharacters (:151-154), spawn failure becomes exit_code=-1 (:154), exclusive child output collision preserves prior content (:155-158), stop_source cancellation yields exit_code 130 for the running AND the never-launched queued job (:159-163), parent cwd unchanged (:164). (b) native/tests/platform/test_primitives.cpp:107-213: argv-not-shell (:107-110), exit status+verbatim streams (:117-121), child-local cwd with spaced path (:124-135), timeout -> process-group SIGKILL 128+SIGKILL with bounded wait (:138-152), 2 MiB stderr flood proving concurrent drain (:156-163), max_output_bytes overflow throws after kill/reap (:166-176), input-domain rejects: empty argv/argv[0], NUL, bad cwd, timeout<=0, nonfinite timeout, zero bound (:178-213); platform::run also driven as the harness for every subprocess test (test_batch.cpp:220-281, test_research_sweep.cpp:42-97). (c) native/tests/integration/test_batch.cpp: expansion order/bool flags/ranges/out-reserved/unknown sections/slugs (:43-150), per-job research-parser validation rejection (:154-166), real serial two-job episode with ordered jsonl records (:200-242), --jobs 0 and -2 exit 2 with NO output directory created (:246-261), explicit --jobs 1 -> workers=1 start_method=in-process + thread_policy echo (:266-305), two-worker results identical to serial (:309-366); native/tests/reference/test_research_sweep.cpp:37-108 covers dry-run listing, parse_seeds ranges, and stub-child return-code propagation into sweep.json; test_acceptance.cpp:244-257 covers CliOptions per-module option allowlists, duplicate flags and alias exclusivity (the bike-validate frontend's cross-mode grammar). (d) native/tests/validation/test_acceptance.cpp: source_fingerprint stability, 64-hex, non-directory rejection (:352-354); validate_dataset rejects synthetic by default and accepts with allow_synthetic (:124-125); calibration_report downgrades a perfect synthetic holdout to parameterized_unvalidated (:129-132); accepted_calibration_status synthetic gate (:131-132); validate_split overlap/whitespace rejection (:133-134); is_synthetic prefix/table (:380-388); write_release_manifest rejection for unvalidated evidence (:190); benchmark_suite jobs!=1 rejection (:229); registry id uniqueness + benchmark discoverability (:223-228). GAPS: (1) no test exercises the SIGINT->grace->SIGKILL ladder's second stage (child ignoring SIGINT); test_plan.cpp:159-163 only covers the first-stage SIGINT exit via batch; (2) no test for the post-reap released-window in ~ChildOwner — the exact defect site is untestable from public API; (3) no cross-compile/SPI test for the add_chdir branches — the _np and SDK<26 paths are compile-only on this host; (4) no sweep test asserting rejection of semantically-invalid passthrough args or of --jobs 0 — both (c) violations are unguarded; (5) no test asserting fingerprint coverage of .inc/.mm/.cu/.in, and no test mutating a source file mid-run to prove source_unchanged_during_run gates fire; (6) no test for interrupted-run checkpoint semantics (partial benchmark -> last checkpoint passed:false) — the progressive writes are code-reviewed but not exercised under kill; (7) capture-only suites (drivetrain comparison, scenario run, reference_matrix, contact_resolution) have no source-edit gate to test even if mutation tests existed; (8) allocation_faults.cpp does not target batch::execute/process_pool critical sections, so 'no heap allocation under mutex' rests on code inspection (pre-sized completed[], index-only write) rather than fault-injection proof.

## 5. Recommended Refinement
Split P20 into independently checkable properties and fix three letter-level mismatches. (1) In process.cpp, set child.released=true immediately when waitpid returns the pid (move the flag write to :310-311, e.g. `reaped=true; child.released=true;`) so no post-reap exception path can re-enter ~ChildOwner; alternatively keep a separate `armed` flag cleared at reap. (2) In sweep.cpp, either call cli::validate_research_arguments after parse_arguments at :45 (matching cli/batch.cpp:34) or explicitly document that sweep relies on per-child validation — and replace the std::max(1,jobs) clamp at :35 with the same rejection bike-batch uses (cli/batch.cpp:24), so --jobs 0/-N exits non-zero before output creation and sweep.json records an honest worker count. (3) In evidence.cpp:96 either include .inc (and .mm/.cu/.in/.hpp.in templates) in the fingerprint selection, or state the contract as 'tracked C++/CMake source files' — currently the property's 'including .inc headers' is factually wrong for argument_contracts.inc and catalog_data.inc. (4) Decide the source-edit gate's intended scope: drivetrain.cpp, scenarios.cpp, reference_matrix and contact_resolution record source_sha256 as provenance only; if 'fail closed on mid-run source edits' is meant lattice-wide, add the unchanged re-comparison to their passed/status terms as benchmark.cpp:247-250 and experiments.cpp:146-147 do, or narrow the property to 'benchmark report types that declare source_unchanged_during_run'. (5) Note benchmark_suite's `passed` does not include unchanged while mechanics_gate_passed/release_gate_passed do — either fold unchanged into `passed` or document the two-tier semantics. (6) For the macOS add_chdir branch, switch the guard to __MAC_OS_X_VERSION_MIN_REQUIRED >= 260000 (deployment target) or add a runtime availability check, since __MAC_OS_X_VERSION_MAX_ALLOWED tracks the SDK, not the target. (7) Test additions worth the budget: a platform::run case whose child ignores SIGINT to prove grace->SIGKILL ordering; a sweep test for invalid passthrough args and --jobs 0; a fingerprint extension-list assertion listing .inc by name; and a fault-injection entry for process_pool's mutex critical section if allocation_faults coverage is desired. Verdict rationale: (a) holds fully; (b),(c),(d) each contain a concrete letter violation (deferred released flag, missing validate_research_arguments call + jobs clamp, .inc exclusion + capture-only suites) inside otherwise sound designs — hence partially_violated rather than violated, since the surrounding systems still fail closed end-to-end in most cases.

# Stage 3 (`R1`) Independent Research Report — `P21`: `Sha256` Single-Finish Latch, Lossless IO Decoders, `SeedSequence::spawn` Overflow Safety & `slsqp` Restoration Status

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Compliance Verdict (`R1`):** `partially_violated`
- **Product Justification Verdict (`R1`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Producers, Mutators, Consumers & Potential Breakers
(a) Sha256: producers/mutators are update() (native/src/io/hash.cpp:13-27) and finish() (hash.cpp:29-48) mutating state_/block_/total_/pending_ (hash.hpp:25-31); consumers: io::sha256 one-shot (hash.cpp:112-123), platform::file_sha256 chunked reader (native/src/platform/files.cpp:207-223), engine-call image identity (native/src/engine_call.cpp:387), state/equilibrium/setup digests (native/src/initialization/{state_file.cpp:81,setup_owner.cpp:138,equilibrium_cache.cpp:203}), recording/duckdb.cpp:516, validation/evidence.cpp:107 — every in-tree caller performs a single finish() and never resumes update(), so the missing latch is latent. (b) FileHandle production boundary: open_exclusive (files.cpp:108-120) and open_regular (files.cpp:122-132) both open with O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC then require_regular post-open fstat (files.cpp:40-53); write_exclusive composes create+write_all+sync+explicit close (files.cpp:177-185); consumers include read_numpy_bytes (native/src/io/npy.cpp:143-150) and file_sha256. Decoders: parse_json/parse_recording_json (json.cpp:1793-1837), parse_npy (npy.cpp:119-134), parse_npz (npz.cpp:99-146), all bounded (json.hpp:26-27, npy.hpp:12-13). NumpyRng consumers: SensorNoiseSource::draw fixed four-call order + dropout (native/src/random/noise_tape.cpp:98-114), make_sensor_tape (native/src/research/rider_random.cpp:19-39), sample_rider five draws (noise_tape.cpp:152-179). (c) spawn() producers/mutators: SeedSequence::spawn (native/src/random/seed_sequence.cpp:243-257) mutating n_children_spawned_ (numpy_compat.hpp:97); only callers issue spawn(2) (noise_tape.cpp:46, rider_random.cpp:24) — the overflow path is unreachable in-tree, hazard is public-API latent. (d) slsqp producer: bike::numerics::slsqp (native/src/numerics/slsqp.cpp:140-222); consumers: rider::allocate restarts×4 ignoring converged/status and re-validating via allocation_residual≤1e-7 (native/src/rider/allocation.cpp:95-116) and linear_feasibility re-verifying the witness (native/src/numerics/linear_feasibility.cpp:83-90) — no in-tree consumer trusts the flag; the contract lives on SlsqpResult's documented status semantics (slsqp.hpp:15-17).

## 2. Sub-Clause Evidence Analysis
(a) VIOLATED: no Streaming→Finalized latch exists. Sha256 has only state_/block_/total_/pending_ members (hash.hpp:25-31) — no finalized flag. update() (hash.cpp:13-27) has no post-finish rejection: after finish(), pending_ retains its post-0x80 position (≤56, or 0 if the length block overflowed, hash.cpp:31-35), so a subsequent update() silently hashes bytes into the already-padded block, producing corrupted digests. finish() (hash.cpp:29-48) is noexcept and idempotent-free: a second call rewrites padding at the stale pending_ and compresses again, returning a different deterministic-but-meaningless digest. Neither misuse is rejected; the claimed 'explicit transition' is absent. Latent: all consumers call finish() exactly once. (b) HOLDS: FileHandle opens are O_NOFOLLOW with post-open fstat S_ISREG validation (files.cpp:108-132 via require_regular 40-53) — the opened object, never a pre-open stat; tests confirm leaf-symlink rejection and /dev/null rejection (tests/platform/test_primitives.cpp:264-303). parse_json is bounded (kMaxJsonBytes=512MiB, kMaxDepth=512 — json.hpp:26-27; enforced json.cpp:1794, 259-261, 274-278), rejects NUL/BOM (json.cpp:1796-1808), runs RapidJSON SAX with kParseNumbersAsStringsFlag so RawNumber stores lexemes verbatim (json.cpp:220-222), rejects duplicate keys (253-254) and unpaired-surrogate UTF-8 (242-245). parse_recording_json adds enforce_finite + fold_tags with exact member-set tag decode (json.cpp:1414-1531, strict b64 339-397, escaped-object duplicate check 1520-1521). recording_json emits sorted keys, verbatim lexemes, duplicate detection on constructed trees, and finite-lexeme validation (json.cpp:1708-1787, 1846-1851). parse_npy validates magic/version/header ≤64KiB, dtype whitelist rejecting pickle/objects (npy.cpp:33-35, 119-134), exact payload-size equality (131-132), ≤16 dims, byte-order-aware bit extraction; typed accessors preserve bit fidelity (bit_cast doubles 95-104, sign-extended integers 105-107, 0/1-only booleans 109, validated UTF-8/UCS4 strings 110-118). parse_npz is a bounded central-directory reader: single-disk only, ≤4096 members, flags mask 0x080e (npz.cpp:120), stored/DEFLATE only, regular-file mode check (123), safe '.npy' names (90-95), duplicate-name rejection (125), ZIP64 extras (126-129), zip-bomb bound expanded≤max(1024,compressed·10000) (130), local/central header consistency, non-overlapping extents (132-135,144), CRC32 verification (139), inflate capped at declared size with trailing-data rejection (44-89). NumpyRng is a statement-for-statement PCG64 port: emulated 128-bit setseq (numpy_compat.cpp:35-75), pcg64_set_seed seeding order (98-115), step-then-XSL-RR next64 (119-123), buffered 32-bit half-lane next32 (127-136), uint64_to_double (139-142); distribution transforms keep NumPy's lane order and stream consumption (distributions.cpp: Ziggurat 308-338, fp-contract-pinned normal/uniform 345-361, Lemire lanes 269-301, bounded_uint64 lane order 367-379, kahan_sum 382-394); SensorNoiseSource preserves the four-call normal order + one dropout uniform even at zero scale/disabled IMU (noise_tape.cpp:98-114). (c) VIOLATED: SeedSequence::spawn has no wraparound guard. The loop bound 'n_children_spawned_ + n_children' (seed_sequence.cpp:246-247) and the counter update 'n_children_spawned_ += n_children' (255) are plain uint32 arithmetic (member type numpy_compat.hpp:97); NumPy's n_children_spawned is an unbounded Python int, so the port silently narrows the domain. Concretely: n_children_spawned_=5 + n_children=UINT32_MAX wraps the bound to 4 → the loop body never runs → empty vector returned while the counter wraps down to 4 → a subsequent spawn reissues already-used spawn-key indices, producing distinct children with identical spawn keys and identical streams (silent aliasing, fails open). spawn(UINT32_MAX) from counter 0 does not wrap the bound but reserve(4294967295) (seed_sequence.cpp:245) relies on incidental length_error/bad_alloc or OOM during ~4 billion push_backs — not a designed fail-closed. Only call sites issue spawn(2) (noise_tape.cpp:46, rider_random.cpp:24), so the defect is latent. (d) PARTIALLY VIOLATED: the restoration block (slsqp.cpp:159-172, comment 160-162: 'diagnostic iterate, never an infeasibility certificate or an optimizer success flag') falls through to the shared exits without a qp.solved precondition: the first success exit (slsqp.cpp:178-179: norm(qp.step)≤ftol && current_violation≤ftol) can fire on a restoration iteration when the iterate is already feasible and the restoration least-squares step is tiny (a≈0 rhs); the second (slsqp.cpp:217-218) can fire when an accepted restoration step lands feasible with flat objective — and restoration steps are preferentially accepted via the violation-reduction clause (slsqp.cpp:193-194). Reachability requires a failed QP at a near-feasible iterate (degenerate Hessian/KKT range), which is narrow but structurally open; notably the BFGS curvature update at line 201 DOES condition on qp.solved, so the code distinguishes restoration elsewhere but not on the success flags. Mitigation: every success exit still requires violation≤ftol, so restoration can never yield an *infeasible* success — the certificate semantics of the contract hold even where the literal 'never sets converged on a restoration step' does not.

## 3. Exceptions, Dependencies & Consequences
Assumptions hold: arithmetic is fixed-width and deterministic; Sha256 wrapping ops are spec-mandated on uint32 (hash.cpp:4). Exceptions/dependencies: (a) the latch absence only matters to API misuse; noexcept signature makes a throwing latch a breaking API change — a flag would need a defined error channel (assert/abort or signature change). total_ is uint64 and wraps at 2^64 bytes (hash.cpp:14) — unreachable practically. (b) recording_json's emit recursion is depth-unbounded for CONSTRUCTED Value trees (parse-side depth is capped at 512); escaped-object emission relies on member-order fidelity of ObjectList (value.hpp:69-84). (c) consequences of the missing guard: (i) empty-child return with counter rollback → spawn-key aliasing → correlated 'independent' RNG streams (silent determinism loss, the worst outcome for seeded fixture parity); (ii) UINT32_MAX reserve attempts ~310 GiB allocation — bad_alloc on macOS, possible overcommit+OOM-kill on Linux rather than a clean domain error. (d) consequence of converged=true on a restoration iteration is bounded: the returned x is within ftol feasibility by construction of the gate, so downstream allocate()/linear_feasibility see no false feasible claims — but any external consumer reading status==0 as 'QP-optimal' gets a restoration iterate instead. linear_feasibility additionally catches only std::runtime_error around slsqp (linear_feasibility.cpp:88-90) — slsqp's invalid_argument paths (slsqp.cpp:141-143, allocation validate) would propagate, a minor but consistent boundary.

## 4. Test Coverage & Gaps
Coverage: Sha256 FIPS vectors incl. 1M-'a' streaming chunks and file-manifest digest (native/tests/io/test_json.cpp:847-896) — no misuse test (update-after-finish, double-finish, or latch assertion). File primitives well covered: exclusive create/empty-dest rejection, leaf vs directory symlink, /dev/null rejection, explicit handle path, hash-after-close, image self-hash (native/tests/platform/test_primitives.cpp:217-316, 412-416). JSON parse/recording codec exercised by checks_json/checks_recording (test_json.cpp up to ~line 845, fixtures). NPY/NPZ round trips incl. big-endian decode, UCS4 strings, bool dtype, truncation/path/CRC corruption and duplicate names (native/tests/initialization/test_transfer.cpp:102-124; native/tests/reference/test_golden_episode_codec.cpp:43-61, 125-127). SeedSequence: spawn(2) fixture, sequential spawn chains, deep-child counters, spawn(0) no-op, entropy-coercion grammar (native/tests/random/test_seeded.cpp:311-432, 781-826) — no overflow/wraparound case; spawn(UINT32_MAX) and cumulative-wrap aliasing untested. NumpyRng: oracle fixture streams (test_seeded.cpp throughout, incl. spawned-child stream equivalence at 750-753) plus validation domains (828+). slsqp: NO direct unit test — exercised only through allocate() call-site tests asserting Allocation.feasible/violation, never SlsqpResult.converged/status (native/tests/reference/test_native_rider_allocation.cpp); the !qp.solved restoration path and the status=0-on-restoration scenario are untested, as is status=6 on a.rows==0.

## 5. Recommended Refinement
(a) Refine statement to 'Sha256 enforces a single-finish protocol': either add an explicit finalized_ latch checked in update()/finish() (needs a non-noexcept error path or assert — breaking noexcept at hash.hpp:19-20), or downgrade the claim to the actual contract 'single-use streaming primitive; misuse is undefined' documented in hash.hpp; add a misuse regression either way. (c) Add a real guard in spawn() before reserve: `if (n_children > std::numeric_limits<std::uint32_t>::max() - n_children_spawned_) throw std::overflow_error(...)`, which also makes the counter honest; testing needs a counter-seeding seam (friend test hook or spawn_key-injected ctor) since reaching UINT32_MAX via real spawns is impractical. (d) Either precondition both success exits on qp.solved (skip convergence return on restoration iterations and let the loop continue/limit out) — matching the comment at slsqp.cpp:160-162 literally — or amend the property to its true invariant: 'converged=true only when violation≤ftol, even on restoration iterations; restoration yields no certificate'. Add a unit test constructing a consistent-but-QP-inconsistent problem (degenerate Hessian at a feasible iterate) asserting no status=0 on restoration, plus direct SlsqpResult status assertions. Overall the property bundles four unrelated invariants; split into per-component properties and mark (a) as a hardening contract rather than a requirement — no doc mandates the latch, while (b)'s lossless/bounded decoder claims ARE confirmed product requirements (docs/RECORDING.md:167-171; files.hpp storage.py contract; numpy_compat.hpp oracle pins) and (d) maps to the documented status contract (slsqp.hpp:15-17).
